I just haven't paid that much attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since these eating changes began?\n\nPatient: I haven't been keeping track of my weight on a scale, but... well, what actually made me come in is that my eating has just gotten all turned around. And then other days, I'll just stand at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets... even though nothing really tastes right to me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Aside from changes in eating, have you lost interest or pleasure in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, my quilting circle, and my book club. But lately... I'll pack my bag for them, and then I just leave it by the door. I just can't make myself care enough to actually go anymore. It's like the enjoyment is just gone.\n\nDoctor: On days when you do not feel depressed, do you still notice the same changes in your eating?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. Like I said before, I haven't been keeping track of it like that. I just know things are off right now, but I couldn't tell you if it happens on days I'm not feeling down. I just haven't noticed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low most days?\n\nPatient: Well... I'd say it's been about a year and two months now. It's just been this low, colorless stretch that's been with me all that time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

And then other days, I'll just stand at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets, even though nothing really tastes right to me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. Most mornings, I wake up with this heavy, low feeling. It's just... like all the color has gone out of the day before I even get dressed.\n\nDoctor: When you eat this way, do you feel unable to stop once you start?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. It's not like I can't stop. I just... I just stand there and eat without really thinking about it. But if the phone rang or I needed to do something, I could stop just fine. It's not out of control, just... mindless, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worried about gaining weight or dissatisfied with your body shape?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, at my age, I'm not too bothered about my shape anymore. I haven't really been worrying about gaining weight or anything like that. It's just the eating itself that's gotten strange, not how I look.\n\nDoctor: Did your eating changes begin after you started feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about the timing, to be honest. I didn't pay that close attention to exactly when the eating changed. It just sort of... happened. I couldn't tell you for certain if it was before or after I started feeling so down.\n\nDoctor: Do your eating changes occur only when you are feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't say for sure. I haven't kept track of it like that. I know I've been feeling down a lot lately, and the eating thing is happening, but I don't know if they only happen at the exact same time. I just haven't paid that much attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since these eating changes began?\n\nPatient: I haven't been keeping track of my weight on a scale, but... well, what actually made me come in is that my eating has just gotten all turned around. Some days I'll realize I forgot to eat lunch completely. And then other days, I'll just stand at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets... even though nothing really tastes right to me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Aside from changes in eating, have you lost interest or pleasure in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, my quilting circle, and my book club. But lately... I'll pack my bag for them, and then I just leave it by the door. I just can't make myself care enough to actually go anymore. It's like the enjoyment is just gone.\n\nDoctor: On days when you do not feel depressed, do you still notice the same changes in your eating?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. Like I said before, I haven't been keeping track of it like that. I just know things are off right now, but I couldn't tell you if it happens on days I'm not feeling down. I just haven't noticed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low most days?\n\nPatient: Well... I'd say it's been about a year and two months now. It's just been this low, colorless stretch that's been with me all that time.\n\nDoctor: How much do these eating changes interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I know I'm not eating like I used to, but... I don't know if it's really stopping me from doing things. It's just sort of happening in the background, I guess. I haven't really thought about it interfering with anything specific.\n\nDoctor: When you skip meals or eat mindlessly, is that mainly because your depression has reduced your appetite or motivation to eat?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't say for sure. I mean... I know I've been feeling low, and I know my eating is out of sorts, but I don't know if one is exactly causing the other. I just haven't really put the two together like that to know if it's a lack of motivation or just not being hungry. I just don't have a clear answer for you on that.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating changes themselves cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they cause me distress. I mean... I know my eating is different now, but it doesn't really upset me or anything. It's just kind of how things are right now. I don't really worry about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or lacking energy during this period?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm getting older, so I'm not running marathons or anything, but I haven't noticed feeling unusually tired. My energy level seems about the same as it usually is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. And then other days, I'll just stand at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets, even though nothing really tastes right to me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. Most mornings, I wake up with this heavy, low feeling. It's just... like all the color has gone out of the day before I even get dressed.\n\nDoctor: When you eat this way, do you feel unable to stop once you start?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. It's not like I can't stop. I just... I just stand there and eat without really thinking about it. But if the phone rang or I needed to do something, I could stop just fine. It's not out of control, just... mindless, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worried about gaining weight or dissatisfied with your body shape?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, at my age, I'm not too bothered about my shape anymore. I haven't really been worrying about gaining weight or anything like that. It's just the eating itself that's gotten strange, not how I look.\n\nDoctor: Did your eating changes begin after you started feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about the timing, to be honest. I didn't pay that close attention to exactly when the eating changed. It just sort of... happened. I couldn't tell you for certain if it was before or after I started feeling so down.\n\nDoctor: Do your eating changes occur only when you are feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't say for sure. I haven't kept track of it like that. I know I've been feeling down a lot lately, and the eating thing is happening, but I don't know if they only happen at the exact same time. I just haven't paid that much attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since these eating changes began?\n\nPatient: I haven't been keeping track of my weight on a scale, but... well, what actually made me come in is that my eating has just gotten all turned around. Some days I'll realize I forgot to eat lunch completely. And then other days, I'll just stand at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets... even though nothing really tastes right to me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Aside from changes in eating, have you lost interest or pleasure in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, my quilting circle, and my book club. But lately... I'll pack my bag for them, and then I just leave it by the door. I just can't make myself care enough to actually go anymore. It's like the enjoyment is just gone.\n\nDoctor: On days when you do not feel depressed, do you still notice the same changes in your eating?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I just haven't noticed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low most days?\n\nPatient: Well... I'd say it's been about a year and two months now. It's just been this low, colorless stretch that's been with me all that time.\n\nDoctor: How much do these eating changes interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I know I'm not eating like I used to, but... I don't know if it's really stopping me from doing things. It's just sort of happening in the background, I guess. I haven't really thought about it interfering with anything specific.\n\nDoctor: When you skip meals or eat mindlessly, is that mainly because your depression has reduced your appetite or motivation to eat?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't say for sure. I mean... I know I've been feeling low, and I know my eating is out of sorts, but I don't know if one is exactly causing the other. I just haven't really put the two together like that to know if it's a lack of motivation or just not being hungry. I just don't have a clear answer for you on that.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating changes themselves cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they cause me distress. I mean... I haven't kept track of it like that. I know I've been feeling down a lot lately, and the eating thing is happening, but I don't know if they only happen at the exact same time. I just haven't paid that much attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since these eating changes began?\n\nPatient: I haven't been keeping track of my weight on a scale, but... well, what actually made me come in is that my eating has just gotten all turned around. Some days I'll realize I forgot to eat lunch completely. And then other days, I'll just stand at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets... even though nothing really tastes right to me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Aside from changes in eating, have you lost interest or pleasure in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, my quilting circle, and my book club. But lately... I'll pack my bag for them, and then I just leave it by the door. I just can't make myself care enough to actually go anymore. It's like the enjoyment is just gone.\n\nDoctor: On days when you do not feel depressed, do you still notice the same changes in your eating?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. Like I said before, I haven't been keeping track of it like that. I just know things are off right now, but I couldn't tell you if it happens on days I'm not feeling down. I just haven't noticed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low most days?\n\nPatient: Well... I'd say it's been about a year and two months now. It's just been this low, colorless stretch that's been with me all that time.\n\nDoctor: How much do these eating changes interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I know I'm not eating like I used to, but... I don't know if it's really stopping me from doing things. It's just sort of happening in the background, I guess. I haven't really thought about it interfering with anything specific.\n\nDoctor: When you skip meals or eat mindlessly, is that mainly because your depression has reduced your appetite or motivation to eat?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't say for sure. I mean... I know I've been feeling low, and I know my eating is out of sorts, but I don't know if one is exactly causing the other. I just haven't really put the two together like that to know if it's a lack of motivation or just not being hungry. I just don't have a clear answer for you on that.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating changes themselves cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they cause me distress. I mean... I know my eating is different now, but it doesn't really upset me or anything. It's just kind of how things are right now. I don't really worry about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or lacking energy during this period?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm getting older, so I'm not running marathons or anything, but I haven't noticed feeling unusually tired. My energy level seems about the same as it usually is.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about dying or killing yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any kind of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But... I do sometimes think that if I just went to sleep and didn't wake up one morning, it might be a relief. Mostly a relief to everyone else, really. But no, I'm not going to do anything to make that happen.\n\nDoctor: Has this low mood been present more days than not for at least two consecutive years?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been that long. It's really just been the last few weeks that I've felt this way. Before that, I felt fine.\n\nDoctor: For how long continuously has this current low mood been present?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think... It hasn't been two years, but it's been going on for a while. I'd say this low, kind of colorless feeling has been with me for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you noticed any change in your sleep?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My sleep has been about the same as it usually is. I haven't noticed any big changes with it.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you had difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I'll just sit there staring at simple things, like trying to decide whether to call the pharmacy or open the mail first. My mind gets so foggy that I usually end up doing neither one. It's just been very hard to focus my thoughts lately.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
